\displaystyle\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right),\mathcal{N}_{\omega},\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}})})\subset\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right)\mathop{\bigcup}\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}^{\circ}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right).

Next, we want to prove the inverse inclusion. For any distribution

\mathbb{Q|}_{Y}\in\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right)\mathop{\bigcup}\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}^{\circ}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right),

there exists
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{1}
such that

\mathcal{W}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}},\mathbb{Q}|_{Y})\leqslant\delta,

or exists
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
such that

\mathcal{W}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}},\mathbb{Q}|_{Y})<\delta.

Assume that the optimal transport between
\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}
and
\mathbb{Q|}_{Y}
is
\pi^{*}|_{Y}
. Under the optimal transport,
\mathbb{Q|}_{Y}
has the following decomposition:

\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathbb{Q}_{i}|_{Y},

where
\mathbb{Q}_{i}|_{Y}
is transferred from
\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}
under the optimal transport. One can verify that for
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}
, there exists
\psi_{\mathbb{Q}}>0
such that
\mathcal{W}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}},\mathbb{Q}|_{{Y}})\leqslant\delta-\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(\frac{\varepsilon}{N}-p_{i})\psi_{\mathbb{Q}}
. Specifically, if
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{1}
, we have
p_{i}=\frac{\varepsilon}{N}
for
i=m+1,...,N
and
\mathcal{W}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}},\mathbb{Q}|_{{Y}})\leqslant\delta=\delta-\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(\frac{\varepsilon}{N}-p_{i})\psi_{\mathbb{Q}}
for any
\psi_{\mathbb{Q}}>0
. Otherwise, if
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
, we have
\mathcal{W}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}},\mathbb{Q}|_{{Y}})\leqslant\delta-\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(\frac{\varepsilon}{N}-p_{i})\psi_{\mathbb{Q}}
for some
\psi_{\mathbb{Q}}>0
, due to
\mathcal{W}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}},\mathbb{Q}|_{{Y}})<\delta
. Then we can construct

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{x}_{i}^{p}}\times\mathbb{Q}_{i}|_{Y}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{x}_{i}}\times\mathbb{Q}_{i}|_{Y}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\frac{1}{N}-\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon})\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(\frac{1}{N}-\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon})\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i}^{p}+\phi_{\mathbb{Q}}^{i}{(\widehat{x}_{i}^{p}-\widehat{x}_{i})},\widehat{y}_{i})},

where
\phi_{\mathbb{Q}}^{i}
satisfies that
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{x}_{i}^{p}+\phi^{i}_{\mathbb{Q}}{(\widehat{x}_{i}^{p}-\widehat{x}_{i})})=d_{i}+\psi^{i}_{\mathbb{Q}}=d_{i}+\psi^{i}_{\mathbb{Q}}
. Note that the conditional distribution of
Y
given
X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)
under the joint distribution
\mathbb{Q}
is
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}
. Moreover, one can verify that
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}
can be represented as

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{P}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon}\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})}

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\frac{1}{N}-\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon})\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(\frac{1}{N}-\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon})\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})},

and using the definition of optimal transport, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}},\mathbb{Q})\leqslant

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon}(d_{i}+\int\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathbb{Q}_{i}|_{Y}(y))

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{x}_{i})+\int\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathbb{Q}_{i}|_{Y}(y))

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{m}(\frac{1}{N}-\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon})(\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{x}_{i})+\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i}))

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(\frac{1}{N}-\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon})(\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{x}_{i}^{p}+\phi_{\mathbb{Q}}{(\widehat{x}_{i}^{p}-\widehat{x}_{i})})+\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})).

And thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}},\mathbb{Q})\leqslant

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon}\int\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathbb{Q}_{i}|_{Y}(y)+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon}\int\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathbb{Q}_{i}|_{Y}(y)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(\frac{1}{N}-\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon})d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(\frac{1}{N}-\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon})\psi_{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathcal{W}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}},\mathbb{Q}|_{Y})+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(\frac{1}{N}-\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon})d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(\frac{1}{N}-\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon})\psi_{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\delta_{0}.

We conclude that for any

\mathbb{Q|}_{Y}\in\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right)\mathop{\bigcup}\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}^{\circ}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right),

there exists
\mathbb{Q}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}
satisfies
\mathbb{Q}\left(X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)\right)\geqslant\varepsilon
such that the conditional distribution of
Y
given
X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)
is
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right)\mathop{\bigcup}\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}^{\circ}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right)\subset\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right),\mathcal{N}_{\omega},\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}})}),

and thus

\displaystyle\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right)\mathop{\bigcup}\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}^{\circ}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right)=\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right),\mathcal{N}_{\omega},\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}})}).

Denote

\mathcal{U_{B}}=\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{1}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right)\mathop{\bigcup}\left(\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}^{\circ}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\right).

From the above discussion, the problem (
7
) is equivalent to

\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{U_{B}}}\rho_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y,\alpha)\right].

We next prove that given
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{U_{B}}}\rho_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y,\alpha)\right]=\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\bigcup\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\rho_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y,\alpha)\right].

(31)

We recall that the risk measure
\rho
and the loss function
\ell
satisfy Assumption
2.2
. Based on Assumption
2.2
, we only need to prove the following claim.

Claim
For any
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)
with
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
and sufficiently large
n\in\mathbb{N}
, denoted by
Y\sim{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}
and
Y_{0}\sim\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}
, there exist
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y_{n}}\in\mathcal{U_{B}}
and
Y_{n}\sim{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y_{n}}
such that
\mathcal{W}(Y,{Y}_{n})\leqslant\frac{1}{n}
.

In fact, if the claim holds, for any
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)
with
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
, we find that
\{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y_{n}}\}\subset\mathcal{U_{B}}
satisfies that
\mathcal{W}({\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y_{n}},{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y})\rightarrow 0
as
n\to 0
and then
\rho\left[\ell(Y_{n},\alpha)\right]\rightarrow\rho\left[\ell(Y,\alpha)\right]
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, due to the OT continuity of
\rho\circ\ell
. Moreover, for any
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)
with
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{1}
, let
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y_{n}}={\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{U_{B}}}\rho_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y,\alpha)\right]-\varepsilon\leqslant\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\bigcup\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\rho_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y,\alpha)\right]\leqslant\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{U_{B}}}\rho_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y,\alpha)\right]+\varepsilon,

and thus the equation (
31
) holds. Next we prove the claim in the two cases:
\delta>0
and
\delta=0
.

(i)

For
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)
with
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
and
\delta>0
, we find that
\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)
is the closure of
\mathbb{B}_{\delta}^{\circ}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)
under the cost
\mathcal{W}
. Thus, there exist
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y_{n}}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}^{\circ}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\subset\mathcal{U_{B}}
and
Y_{n}\sim{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y_{n}}
such that
\mathcal{W}(Y,{Y}_{n})\leqslant\frac{1}{n}
for sufficiently large
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

(ii)

For
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)
with
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
and
\delta=0
, we find that
\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)
is a single distribution set and
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}
. We consider the following two cases.

(iia)

If
\delta_{0}>\delta_{min}
, from the proof of Proposition
1
, there exist
(p^{*},\delta^{*})\in\mathcal{V}
such that
\delta^{*}>0
. Let
Y\sim\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}
and
Y^{*}\sim\sum_{i=1}^{N}p^{*}_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}
. One can verify that
\mathcal{W}(Y,Y^{*})\leqslant\max_{i,j=1,...,N}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},\widehat{y}_{j}):=D
. For
n\in\mathbb{N}
satisfies that
nD\geqslant 1
, let
p^{n}=(1-\frac{1}{nD})p+\frac{1}{nD}p^{*}
and
\delta^{n}=(1-\frac{1}{nD})\delta+\frac{1}{nD}\delta^{*}
. Due to the convexity of
\mathcal{V}
, we have
(p^{n},\delta^{n})\in\mathcal{V}
and thus
\sum_{i=1}^{N}p^{n}_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\in\mathcal{U_{B}}
because
\delta^{*}>0
and then
\delta_{n}>0
. Let
Y_{n}\sim\sum_{i=1}^{N}p^{n}_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}
. Then we find that
\mathcal{W}(Y,{Y}_{n})\leqslant\frac{1}{nD}\mathcal{W}(Y,{Y}^{*})\leqslant\frac{1}{n}
.

(iib)

If
\delta_{0}=\delta_{min}
, we claim that for every
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
,
\delta<0
. Note that
\delta_{0}
can take the smallest radius
\delta_{min}
if and only if
\frac{N-m}{N}\leqslant\omega_{2}
. In fact, if
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
, there exists
\varepsilon
such that
(p,\delta,\varepsilon)\in\mathcal{V}_{0}
, and exists
i\in\{m+1,..,N\}
such that
p_{i}<\frac{\varepsilon}{N}
. Note that
\delta=\varepsilon\left(\delta_{0}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}d_{i}\right)-\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}p_{i}d_{i}
. If there exist
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
satisfies that
\delta\geqslant 0
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{0}\geqslant

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(\frac{1}{N}-\frac{p_{i}}{\varepsilon})d_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{Np_{i}}{\varepsilon}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(1-\frac{Np_{i}}{\varepsilon})d_{i}\right)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}v_{i}^{\prime}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(1-v_{i}^{\prime})d_{i}\right)

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{i}\in[0,1],i=1,...,N,\\
1/N\sum_{i=1}^{N}v_{i}\in[\omega_{1},\omega_{2}]\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}v_{i}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(1-v_{i})d_{i}\right)=\delta_{min},

where the strict inequality follows from that we can always find
\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{m}v_{i}^{\prime}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(1-v_{i}^{\prime})d_{i}>\sum_{i=1}^{m}v_{i}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(1-v_{i})d_{i}
. In fact, for
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
, we find that
v_{i}^{\prime}
s satisfy
v_{i}^{\prime}\in[0,1],i=1,\ldots,N
,
1/N\sum_{i=1}^{N}v_{i}^{\prime}\in\left[\omega_{1},\omega_{2}\right]
, and there exists
i\in\{m+1,\ldots,N\}
such that
v_{i}^{\prime}<1
. Without loss of generality, we assume
v_{i}^{\prime}=1
for
i=m+1,...,k
and
v_{i}^{\prime}<1
for
i=k+1,...,N
. If
1/N\sum_{i=1}^{N}v_{i}^{\prime}\leqslant\frac{N-m}{N}
, let
v_{i}=0
for
i=1,..,m
and
v_{i}=1
for
i=m+1,...,N
. Otherwise, let
v_{i}=v_{i}^{\prime}-\Delta_{i}\geqslant 0
for
i=1,..,m
and
v_{i}=1
for
i=m+1,...,N
, where
\Delta_{i}\geqslant 0
and
\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Delta_{i}=\sum_{i=k+1}^{N}(1-v_{i}^{\prime})
. We find
\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
satisfies that
v_{i}\in[0,1],i=1,...,N,1/N\sum_{i=1}^{N}v_{i}\in[\omega_{1},\omega_{2}]
and such
\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
must exists due to that
\frac{N-m}{N}\leqslant\omega_{2}
. Under the above construction of
\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{N}
, we have
\sum_{i=1}^{m}v_{i}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(1-v_{i})d_{i}<\sum_{i=1}^{m}v_{i}^{\prime}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}(1-v_{i}^{\prime})d_{i}
. The above strict inequality contradicts the assumption
\delta_{0}=\delta_{min}
and thus we have that for every
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
,
\delta<0
. Thus,
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)
is an empty set for every
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{2}
.

Combining the above two cases, we complete the proof of claim and thus complete the proof of Theorem
3
.∎

Proof of Proposition
4
.

To obtain the sufficient and necessary feasible condition of problem (
9
), we only need to find the sufficient and necessary feasible condition of problem (
10
) and thus only need to find the sufficient and necessary condition such that the ambiguity set
\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0}),\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})})
is not empty. Note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0}),\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})})=\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)

from the proof of Theorem
6
. Thus,
\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0}),\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})})
is not empty if and only if there exists
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}
such that
\delta=\delta_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}\geqslant 0
. One can verify that there exists
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}
such that
\delta\geqslant 0
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{i}\in[0,\frac{1}{N\varepsilon}],i=1,...,N,\\
\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\delta_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}\geqslant 0,

and thus equivalent to

\displaystyle\delta_{0}\geqslant

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{i}\in[0,\frac{1}{N\varepsilon}],i=1,...,N,\\
\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{i}\in[0,1],i=1,...,N,\\
\sum_{i=1}^{N}v_{i}=N\varepsilon\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{N\varepsilon}\sum_{i=1}^{m}v_{i}d_{i}=\delta_{min}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
6
.

For problem (
9
), note that
\mathbb{Q}
is the joint distribution of
(X,Y)
and the objective function is only related to
Y
. Then we can reformulate the inner problem of problem (
9
) as

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{{Y}}\in\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0}),\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})})}\rho_{\mathbb{Q}|_{{Y}}}\left[\ell(Y,\alpha)\right],

(32)

where
\mathbb{Q}|_{{Y}}
is the marginal distribution of
Y
and
\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0}),\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})})
is the marginal distribution set on
\mathcal{Y}
, which is defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0}),\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})})=\Bigg\{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}:

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\text{ is the marginal distribution of }\mathbb{Q}\text{ on }\mathcal{Y},

\displaystyle\text{ where }\mathbb{Q}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})\text{ satisfies that }\mathbb{Q}\left(X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0})\right)=1\Bigg\}.

Thus, we only need to prove that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0}),\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})})=\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right).

For any
\mathbb{Q}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})
, based on definition of the trimming set and the aforementioned notifications, we can find that there exists
p
satisfies that given
\varepsilon=\min_{\beta\in\mathcal{N}_{\omega}}\beta
,
p_{i}\in[0,\frac{1}{N\varepsilon}],i=1,...,N,\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}=1
, such that
\mathcal{W}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})},\mathbb{Q}\right)\leqslant\delta_{0}
. Furthermore, if
\mathbb{Q}(X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0}))=1
, assume that the optimal transport between
\mathbb{Q}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})}
is
\pi
.
Then we can decompose
\mathbb{Q}
as
\mathbb{Q}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathbb{Q}^{i}
, where
\mathbb{Q}^{i}
is transferred by
\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})}
under the optimal transport
\pi
. Similar to the proof the Theorem
3
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})},\mathbb{Q})=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}\int\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(\widehat{x}_{i},x)+\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathbb{Q}_{i}(x,y)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}p_{i}\int\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(\widehat{x}_{i},x)+\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathbb{Q}_{i}(x,y)

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}\int d_{i}+\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathbb{Q}_{i}(x,y)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}p_{i}\int\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{x}_{i})+\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathbb{Q}_{i}(x,y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\int\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathbb{Q}_{i}|_{Y}(y)+\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}

\displaystyle\geqslant

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}},\mathbb{Q}|_{Y})+\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}.

Rearranging the above inequality, we find that the marginal distribution on
\mathcal{Y}
of
\mathbb{Q}
satisfies that

\mathcal{W}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}},\mathbb{Q}|_{Y})\leqslant\mathcal{W}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})},\mathbb{Q})-\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}\leqslant\delta.

And thus,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0}),\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})})\subset\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right).

Next, we prove the inverse containment. For any

\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right),

there exists
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}
such that
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)
. Assume the optimal transport between
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}
and
\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}
is
\pi|_{Y}
. Then we can decompose it as
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathbb{Q}^{i}|_{Y}
, where
\mathbb{Q}^{i}|_{Y}
is transferred by
\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}
under the optimal transport
\pi|_{Y}
. Then we can construct a distribution
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{x}_{i}^{p}}\times\mathbb{Q}^{i}|_{Y}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{x}_{i}}\times\mathbb{Q}^{i}|_{Y}
and verify that the marginal distribution on
\mathcal{Y}
of
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}
is
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{(\widehat{x}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i})},\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}\right)\leqslant

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}\int\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(\widehat{x}_{i},x)+\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{x}_{i}^{p}}(x)\times\mathbb{Q}^{i}|_{Y}(y)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}p_{i}\int\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(\widehat{x}_{i},x)+\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{x}_{i}}(x)\times\mathbb{Q}^{i}|_{Y}(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\int\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(\widehat{y}_{i},y)~d\mathbb{Q}^{i}|_{Y}(y)+\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}},\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\delta_{0}.

From the above inequality and the construction of
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}
, we have
\widetilde{\mathbb{Q}}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})
and
\mathbb{Q}\left(X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0})\right)=1
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)\subset\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0}),\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})}),

and thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}({\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0}),\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}};\mathcal{N}_{\omega})})=\mathop{\bigcup}\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right).

∎

EC.2.2. Proofs of Section
3

Proof of Proposition
7
.

Suppose
\rho_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}
is a law-invariant risk measure that is concave in distribution, then
g(\alpha,(p,\delta))
is concave in
(p,\delta)
. In fact, one can verify that

\displaystyle g(\alpha,(p,\delta))=

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left\{\rho_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y,\alpha)\right]+\sigma({\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y},p,\delta|\mathbb{B}_{\delta}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}))\right\}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}H({\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y},p,\delta),

where
\sigma({\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y},p,\delta|\mathbb{B}_{\delta}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}))
is the indicator function of
\mathbb{B}_{\delta}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}})
and
\mathbb{B}_{\delta}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}})
is a convex set of
({\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y},p,\delta)
. Thus, we find that
H({\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y},p,\delta)
is jointly concave in
({\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y},p,\delta)
and then
g(\alpha,(p,\delta))
is jointly concave in
(p,\delta)
by taking the supremum of
H({\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y},p,\delta)
in
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}
. Moreover, suppose
\rho_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\circ\ell
is convex in
\alpha
. We find that

g(\alpha,(p,\delta))=\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}})}\rho_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y,\alpha)\right]
is a supremum of convex functions with respect to
\alpha
and thus
g(\alpha,(p,\delta))
is convex in
\alpha
.∎

Proof of Lemma
9
.

We begin by introducing an auxiliary lemma that will be used throughout the proof.

Lemma 28
.

Assume
Y_{n}\in L^{q}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{n}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
Y\in L^{q}\sim\mathbb{Q}
such that
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(Yn,Y)\right]=\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, where
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}^{q}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
with
q\in[1,\infty)
. If
\ell
is continuous and satisfies that there exists some
C>0
such that
|\ell(x)|\leqslant C(1+|x|^{q})
, given
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]
as
n\to\infty
.

Proof of Lemma
28
.

In the following proof, denote
\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})
as
\mathcal{W}_{q}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})
,
||\cdot||_{\mathcal{Y}}
as
||\cdot||
and
||\cdot||_{*}
as the dual norm of
||\cdot||
. We first prove that
\{\|Y_{n}\|^{q}\}_{n}
is uniformly integrable, that is
\lim_{M\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{n}\mathbb{E}[\|Y_{n}\|^{q}\mathrm{I}_{\{\|Y_{n}\|^{q}>M\}}]=0
. From the assumption that
\mathcal{W}_{q}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, we have these two facts: (i)
\mathbb{Q}_{n}\implies\mathbb{Q}
(weak convergence); (ii)
\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y_{n}\|^{q}\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y\|^{q}\right]
as
n\to\infty
. One can verify that
\mathcal{W}_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})\leqslant\mathcal{W}_{q}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})
and thus
\mathcal{W}_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Using the Kantorovich–Rubinstein duality for
\mathcal{W}_{1}
, for any
1
-Lipschitz continuous function
f
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}_{n}}\left[f(Y_{n})\right]\rightarrow\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[f(Y)\right]
. This implies (i). To see (ii), using triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y_{n}\|^{q}\right]\right)^{1/q}-\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y\|^{q}\right]\right)^{1/q}\right|\leqslant\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y_{n}-Y\|^{q}\right]\right)^{1/q}=\mathcal{W}_{q}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})\to 0\text{ as }n\to\infty.

Hence, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y_{n}\|^{q}\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y\|^{q}\right]
as
n\to\infty
and conclude the proof of (ii). Let
g(y)=||y||^{q}
and
g_{M}(y)=\min\{g(y),M\}
with
M>0
. Due to that
g_{M}
is bounded and continuous, by (i), we have
\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y)\right]
as
n\to\infty
. Let
T_{n}(M)=\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y_{n}\|^{q}\mathrm{I}_{\{\|Y_{n}\|^{q}>M\}}\right]
with
M>0
. Then
T_{n}(M)=\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]+M\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{I}_{\{g(Y_{n})>M\}}\right]
. If

\displaystyle\sup_{n}T_{n}(M)\to 0\text{ as }M\to\infty,

(33)

by the definition of uniformly integrable, we conclude that
\{\|Y_{n}\|^{q}\}_{n}
is uniformly integrable. To prove (
33
), we only need to prove the following claim.

Claim
(i) and (ii) imply that
\sup_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]\to 0
as
M\to\infty
.

In fact, we first note that
M\mathrm{I}_{\{g>M\}}\leqslant 2(g-g_{\frac{M}{2}})
. If the claim holds, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{n}T_{n}(M)=

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]+M\mathbb{E}\left[\mathrm{I}_{\{g(Y_{n})>M\}}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]+2\left(\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{\frac{M}{2}}(Y_{n})\right]\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]\right\}+\sup_{n}\left\{2\left(\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{\frac{M}{2}}(Y_{n})\right]\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle 0\text{ as }M\to\infty.

Thus, we have
\{\|Y_{n}\|^{q}\}_{n}
is uniformly integrable. Next, we prove the claim. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
M>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]\leqslant\left|\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y)\right]\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y)\right]\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]\right|.

By (ii), given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
N_{1}
such that for any
n\geqslant N_{1}
, we have
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y)\right]\right|<\varepsilon/3
. Using (i) and the fact that
g_{M}
is bounded and continuous, given
\varepsilon>0
, for any
M>0
, there exists
N_{2}(M)
such that for any
n\geqslant N_{2}(M)
, we have
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]\right|<\varepsilon/3
. Note that
g_{M}\nearrow g
as
M\to\infty
and
g\in L^{1}
(
Y_{n}\in L^{q}
). By monotone convergence theorem, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y)\right]\searrow 0
as
M\to\infty
. Thus, given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
M_{0}>0
such that for any
M\geqslant M_{0}
, we have
\left|\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y)\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y)\right]\right|<\varepsilon/3
. Combining the above cases, we have the following result. Fix any
M\geqslant M_{0}
. Let
N=\max\{N_{1},N_{2}(M)\}
. Then for any
n\geqslant N
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]<\varepsilon
. Moreover, for any
1\leqslant n\leqslant N
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]\searrow 0
as
M\to\infty
by monotone convergence theorem. Thus, given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
M_{1}
such that for
M\geqslant M_{1}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]\leqslant\varepsilon
with
1\leqslant n\leqslant N
. Then, for
M\geqslant\max\{M_{0},M_{1}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]=

\displaystyle\max\{\max_{1\leqslant n\leqslant N}\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right],\sup_{n\geqslant N}\mathbb{E}\left[g(Y_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[g_{M}(Y_{n})\right]\}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle 0\text{ as }M\to 0.

Thus, we conclude the proof of that
\{\|Y_{n}\|^{q}\}_{n}
is uniformly integrable. In the following, we are going to prove the following two facts.

(a)

Given
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}
,
\{\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)\}_{n}
is uniformly integrable.

(b)

Given
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}
,
\{\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)\}_{n}\to\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)
in probability.

Because
|\ell(x)|\leqslant C(1+|x|^{q})
with
C>0
, we have
|\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)|\leqslant C(1+|Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha|^{q})\leqslant C(1+\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}\|Y_{n}\|^{q})
. Then
|\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)|\mathrm{I}_{\{|\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)|>M\}}\leqslant C(1+\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}\|Y_{n}\|^{q})\mathrm{I}_{\{C(1+\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}\|Y_{n}\|^{q})>M\}}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[|\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)|\mathrm{I}_{\{|\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)|>M\}}\right]\leqslant

\displaystyle\sup_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[C(1+\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}\|Y_{n}\|^{q})\mathrm{I}_{\{C(1+\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}\|Y_{n}\|^{q})>M\}}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sup_{n}C\mathbb{E}\left[C\mathrm{I}_{\{(1+\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}\|Y_{n}\|^{q})>M\}}\right]+\sup_{n}C\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y_{n}\|^{q}\mathrm{I}_{\{\|Y_{n}\|^{q}>\frac{M-C}{C\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}}\}}\right]

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle 0\text{ as }M\to\infty.

We complete the proof of (a). To see (b), due to (ii), we find that
Y_{n}\to Y
in
L^{q}
. Then
Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha\to Y^{\top}\alpha
in
L^{q}
and thus in probability. Because of
\ell
is continuous, we obtain
\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)\to\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)
in probability. Combining (a) and (b), using Vitali’s convergence theorem, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[|\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)-\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)|\right]\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, and thus
\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]
as
n\to\infty
.
∎

We are now ready to complete the proof of Lemma
9
.

Continued proof of Lemma
9

Assume
Y_{n}\in L^{q}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{n}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
Y\in L^{q}\sim\mathbb{Q}
such that
\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(Yn,Y)\right]=\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, where
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}^{q}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
with
q\in[1,\infty)
. We consider the following risk measure case by case.

(i)

\rho(Y)=\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]
. One can verify that
\ell
in Propositions
10
and
11
is continuous and satisfies that there exists
C>0
such that
|\ell(x)|\leqslant C(1+|x|^{q})
. Applying Lemma
28
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]
as
n\to\infty
if
\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. We conclude that
\rho
is OT-continuity.

(ii)

\rho(Y)=\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]
. Because
\ell(z,t):\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
in Propositions
13
and
14
is convex and level-bounded in
t
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
, the infimum is attained. Thus for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
t_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\left|\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]-\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right|\leqslant\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha,t_{n})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t_{n})\right]\right|.

(34)

One can verify that
\ell
in Propositions
13
and
14
is continuous and satisfies that there exists
C>0
such that
|\ell(x)|\leqslant C(1+|x|^{q})
. Applying Lemma
28
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha,t_{n})\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha,t_{n})\right]
as
n\to\infty
if
\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Thus, by inequality (
34
), we have
\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\to\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]
as
n\to\infty
. We conclude that
\rho
is OT-continuity.

(iii)

\rho(Y)=\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{t+\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\ell^{q}(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right)^{1/q}\right\}
. Because
t+\ell(z,t):\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
in Propositions
17
and
18
is convex and level-bounded in
t
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
, the infimum is attained. This applies that there exists
t_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\left|\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\left\{t+\mathbb{E}\left[\ell^{q}(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]^{1/q}\right\}-\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\left\{t+\mathbb{E}\left[\ell^{q}(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]^{1/q}\right\}\right|

(35)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\ell^{q}(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha,t_{n})\right]^{1/q}-\mathbb{E}\left[\ell^{q}(Y^{\top}\alpha,t_{n})\right]^{1/q}\right|.

(36)

One can verify that
\ell^{q}
in Propositions
13
and
14
is continuous and satisfies that there exists
C>0
such that
|\ell^{q}(x)|\leqslant C(1+|x|^{q})
. Applying Lemma
28
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\ell^{q}(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha,t_{n})\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[\ell^{q}(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha,t_{n})\right]
as
n\to\infty
if
\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Thus, by inequality (
35
), we have
\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\left\{t+\mathbb{E}\left[\ell^{q}(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]^{1/q}\right\}\to\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\left\{t+\mathbb{E}\left[\ell^{q}(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]^{1/q}\right\}
as
n\to\infty
. We conclude that
\rho
is OT-continuity.

(iv)

\rho(Y)=\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{E}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}
, where
u
is convex and increasing and
l
is a fixed constant in the interior of the range of
u
.
One can verify that
u
in Propositions
19
and
21
, and Remark
20
is continuous and satisfies that there exists
C>0
such that
|u(x)|\leqslant C(1+|x|^{q})
. Applying Lemma
28
, for every
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[u(-Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]
as
n\to\infty
if
\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q})\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Because
l
is a fixed constant in the interior of the range of
u
, the sublevel set
\left\{\kappa:\mathbb{E}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}
is nonempty, closed and bounded-below interval, that is
\left\{\kappa:\mathbb{E}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}=[\rho(Y),\infty)=[\rho(\mathbb{Q}),\infty)
, where
\rho(Y)=\rho(\mathbb{Q})\in\mathbb{R}
, and for any
\varepsilon>0
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\rho(\mathbb{Q})-\varepsilon)\right]<l
. Moreover, by the definition of shortfall risk measure, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\rho(\mathbb{Q})+\varepsilon)\right]>l
for any
\varepsilon>0
. Because of that
\mathbb{E}\left[u(-Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\to\mathbb{E}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]
as
n\to\infty
, for sufficiently large
n
, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[u(-Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha-\rho(\mathbb{Q})-\varepsilon)\right]<l
and
\mathbb{E}\left[u(-Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha-\rho(\mathbb{Q})+\varepsilon)\right]>l
. Thus,
\rho(\mathbb{Q}_{n})\in(\rho(\mathbb{Q})-\varepsilon,\rho(\mathbb{Q})+\varepsilon]
for sufficiently large
n
. Letting
\varepsilon
decrease to
0
yields
\rho(\mathbb{Q}_{n})\rightarrow\rho(\mathbb{Q})
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. We conclude that
\rho
is OT-continuity.

(v)

\rho(Y)=\rho^{h}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]
, where
h
is a convex distortion function. Let
\ell:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a convex and Lipschitz continuous function. By Hölder inequality and the Lipschitz continuous of
\ell
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\rho^{h}\left[\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)\right]-\rho^{h}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|h_{-}^{\prime}\|_{q^{*}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)-\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|h_{-}^{\prime}\|_{q^{*}}\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\|\alpha\|_{*}\mathbb{E}\left[\|Y_{n}-Y\|^{q}\right]^{1/q}

\displaystyle=\|h_{-}^{\prime}\|_{q^{*}}\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\|\alpha\|_{*}\mathcal{W}(\mathbb{Q}_{n},\mathbb{Q}).

Following the above inequality, we have
\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y_{n}^{\top}\alpha)\right]\rightarrow\mathbb{E}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, and thus
\rho
is OT-continuity.

∎

EC.2.2.1. Proofs of Section
3.1

Proof of Proposition
10
.

(i)

Let
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}
,
\forall y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
and
||\cdot||_{*}
be the dual norm of
||\cdot||_{\mathcal{Y}}
. Applying the equation (
26
), we find that the problem (
15
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]=\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)+\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\delta\|{\alpha}\|_{*}.

Let
p^{\prime}=(p,\delta)
and

f(p^{\prime})=f(p,\delta)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)+\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\delta\|{\alpha}\|_{*}.

Using the Fenchel duality theory, we can reformulate the above inner supremum problem as

\displaystyle\sup_{p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)+\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\delta\|{\alpha}\|_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{p^{\prime}}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)+\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\delta\|{\alpha}\|_{*}-\sigma(p^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{V}_{+})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{v}\left\{\sigma^{*}(v\mid\mathcal{V}_{+})-f_{*}(v)\right\},

where
\sigma^{*}(v\mid\mathcal{V}_{+})
is the convex conjugate function of
\sigma(p^{\prime}|\mathcal{V}_{+})
,
and
f_{*}(v)
is the concave conjugate function of
f(p^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle f_{*}(v)=\inf_{p^{\prime}\geqslant 0}v^{\top}p^{\prime}-\nu(\alpha)^{\top}p^{\prime}=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }v-\nu(\alpha)\geqslant 0,\\
-\infty&\text{ else, }\end{cases}

with
\nu(\alpha)^{\top}=(\ell(\widehat{y}_{1}^{\top}\alpha),\dots,\ell(\widehat{y}_{N}^{\top}\alpha),\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)||\alpha||_{*})\in\mathbb{R}^{N+1}.
Thus, we can reformulate the problem (
15
) as

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha,v}\sigma^{*}(v\mid\mathcal{V}_{+})

\displaystyle~\text{s.t.}~\alpha\in\mathcal{A},v-\nu(\alpha)\geqslant 0.

(ii)

Let
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}^{q}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
and
q\in(1,\infty)
.

(1)

Applying the result in Theorem
24
, we find that the problem (
15
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]=\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}\|{\alpha}\|_{*}.

Let
g(p^{\prime})=\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}\|{\alpha}\|_{*}.
Using the Fenchel duality theory, we can reformulate the above inner supremum problem as

\displaystyle\sup_{p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}\|{\alpha}\|_{*}=\inf_{v}\left\{\sigma^{*}(v\mid\mathcal{V}_{+})-g_{*}(v)\right\},

where
\sigma^{*}(v\mid\mathcal{V}_{+})
is the convex conjugate function of
\sigma(p^{\prime}|\mathcal{V}_{+})
,
and
g_{*}(v)
is the concave conjugate function of
g(p^{\prime})
, defined as

\displaystyle g_{*}(v)=

\displaystyle\inf_{p^{\prime}\geqslant 0}v^{\top}p^{\prime}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)-C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}\|{\alpha}\|_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}-C_{q}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{*}^{\frac{q}{q-1}}&\text{ if }v-\nu^{\prime}(\alpha)\geqslant 0,\\
-\infty&\text{ else, }\end{cases}

with
C_{q}=\left(q^{\frac{1}{1-q}}-q^{\frac{q}{1-q}}\right)C^{\frac{q}{q-1}}
, and
\nu^{\prime}(\alpha)^{\top}=(\ell(\widehat{y}_{1}^{\top}\alpha),\dots,\ell(\widehat{y}_{N}^{\top}\alpha),0)\in\mathbb{R}^{N+1}
. Thus, we can reformulate the problem (
15
) as

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha,v}~\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})+C_{q}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{*}^{\frac{q}{q-1}}

\displaystyle~\text{s.t.}~\alpha\in\mathcal{A},~v-\nu^{\prime}(\alpha)\geqslant 0,

(2)

Applying the result in Theorem
25
, we can reformulate the problem (
15
) as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[\ell^{q}(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left(\left(\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell^{q}(Y,\alpha)\right]\right)^{1/q}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}||\alpha||_{*}\right)^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\left(\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell^{q}(Y,\alpha)\right]\right)^{1/q}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}||\alpha||_{*}\right)^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\left(\sup_{p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell^{q}(Y,\alpha)\right]\right)^{1/q}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}||\alpha||_{*}\right)^{q}.

Next, we focus on solving the following problem

\displaystyle\sup_{p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell^{q}(Y,\alpha)\right]\right)^{1/q}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}||\alpha||_{*}.

Let
h(p^{\prime})=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\ell^{q}(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}\|{\alpha}\|_{*}.
Using the Fenchel duality theory, we can reformulate the above inner supremum problem as

\displaystyle\sup_{p^{\prime}}\left\{h(p^{\prime})-\sigma(p^{\prime}\mid\mathcal{V}_{+})\right\}=\inf_{v}\left\{\sigma^{*}(v\mid\mathcal{V}_{+})-h_{*}(v)\right\},

where
\sigma^{*}(v\mid\mathcal{V}_{+})
is the convex conjugate function of
\sigma(p^{\prime}|\mathcal{V}_{+})
,
and
h_{*}(v)
is the concave conjugate function of
h(p^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle h_{*}(v)=

\displaystyle\inf_{p^{\prime}\geqslant 0}v^{\top}p^{\prime}-h(p^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup-C_{q}^{1}y^{\frac{1}{1-q}}-C_{q}v^{2}_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v^{2}_{N+1}}\right\|_{*}^{\frac{q}{q-1}}

\displaystyle~\text{s.t.}~v=y\cdot\nu^{\prime\prime}(\alpha)+v^{2},~y\geqslant 0,~v^{2}\geqslant 0,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup-C_{q}^{1}y^{\frac{1}{1-q}}-C_{q}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{*}^{\frac{q}{q-1}}

\displaystyle~\text{s.t.}~v-y\cdot\nu^{\prime\prime}(\alpha)\geqslant 0,~y\geqslant 0,

where
C_{q}=C^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\left(q^{\frac{1}{1-q}}-q^{\frac{q}{1-q}}\right)
,
C_{q}^{1}=q^{\frac{1}{1-q}}-q^{\frac{q}{1-q}}
, and

\displaystyle\nu^{\prime\prime}(\alpha)^{\top}=(\ell^{q}(\widehat{y}_{1}^{\top}\alpha),...,\ell^{q}(\widehat{y}_{N}^{\top}\alpha),0)\in\mathbb{R}^{N+1}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\left(\sup_{p^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell^{q}(Y,\alpha)\right]\right)^{1/q}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}||\alpha||_{*}\right)^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\left(\inf_{v}\left\{\sigma^{*}(v\mid\mathcal{V}_{+})-h_{*}(v)\right\}\right)^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},v}\left(\sigma^{*}(v\mid\mathcal{V}_{+})-h_{*}(v)\right)^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha,v,y}\left[\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})+C_{q}^{1}y^{\frac{1}{1-q}}+C_{q}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{*}^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\right]^{q}

\displaystyle~~\text{s.t.}~\alpha\in\mathcal{A},~v-y\cdot\nu^{\prime\prime}(\alpha)\geqslant 0,~y\geqslant 0.

Thus, we obtain the desired result.

∎

The following result, which reduces the worst-case expectation problem via the projection property of
Wu et al. 2022
, will be instrumental in simplifying subsequent proofs.

Theorem 29
.

Let
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}^{q}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
, with
q\in(1,\infty]
. Let
1/q+1/q^{*}=1
. Assume
\ell:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is convex and satisfies
\ell(x)-\ell(x_{0})\leqslant L|x-x_{0}|^{q}+M,x\in\mathbb{R}
for some
L,M>0
, and
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, for any distribution
F_{0}
, and
\delta\geqslant 0
. we have

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}(F_{0})}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}_{F_{0}}\left[\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\ell(y)-\lambda|y-Y^{\top}\alpha|^{q}\right\}\right]+\lambda\delta\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}

(37)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\eta\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}_{F_{0}}\left[\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot{Y}^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\}\right]+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}}{\eta^{q-1}}.

(38)

Moreover, we also find that problems (
37
) and (
38
) are convex optimization problems.

Proof of Theorem
29
.

By the projection result in Proposition 1 of
Wu et al. 2022
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}(F_{0})}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]=

\displaystyle\sup_{\left\{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y^{\top}\alpha}:{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}(F_{0})\right\}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y^{\top}\alpha}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y^{\top}\alpha}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta\|\alpha\|_{*}}(F_{0}|_{Y^{\top}\alpha})}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y^{\top}\alpha}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right].

Using the strong duality result of Theorem 1 in
Gao and Kleywegt 2023
, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y^{\top}\alpha}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta\|\alpha\|_{*}}(F_{0}|_{Y^{\top}\alpha})}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y^{\top}\alpha}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]=\inf_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{F_{0}}\left[\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\ell(y)-\lambda|y-Y^{\top}\alpha|^{q}\right\}\right]+\lambda\delta\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}\right\}.

Thus the first equality holds. Now we focus on the second equality. Since
\ell
is a real-valued convex function on
\mathbb{R}
, we have
\ell=\ell^{**}
, i.e.,
\ell(y)=\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{zy-\ell^{*}(z)\right\}
for all
y\in\mathbb{R}
. Using this relation, (
37
) can be reformulated as follows.

\displaystyle\inf_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}_{F_{0}}\left[\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\ell(y)-\lambda\left|y-Y^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q}\right\}\right]+\lambda\delta\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}_{F_{0}}\left[\sup_{\Delta\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\ell\left(Y^{\top}\alpha+\Delta\right)-\lambda|\Delta|^{q}\right\}\right]+\lambda\delta\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}_{F_{0}}\left[\sup_{\Delta\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\left(Y^{\top}\alpha+\Delta\right)-\ell^{*}(z)\right\}-\lambda|\Delta|^{q}\right\}\right]+\lambda\delta\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}_{F_{0}}\left[\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot Y^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\sup_{\Delta\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\Delta-\lambda|\Delta|^{q}\right\}\right\}\right]+\lambda\delta\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}_{F_{0}}\left[\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot Y^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\left(\frac{1}{\lambda q}\right)^{1/(q-1)}\left(1-\frac{1}{q}\right)|z|^{q/(q-1)}\right\}\right]+\lambda\delta\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}_{F_{0}}\left[\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot Y^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\left(\frac{1}{\lambda}\right)^{q^{*}-1}\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\}\right]+\lambda\delta\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}.

By setting
\eta=(1/\lambda)^{1/(q-1)}
, the above equation reduces to

\inf_{\eta\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}_{F_{0}}\left[\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot Y^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\}\right]+\delta\cdot\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}}{\eta^{q-1}}.

Hence, we have verified that (
37
) and (
38
) are equivalent. Let us now prove that (
38
) is a convex optimization problem. The first term in (
38
) is convex since the mapping
\eta\mapsto z\cdot Y^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+

\eta|z|^{q}/\left(qp^{q-1}\right)
is convex for any fixed
z\in\mathbb{R}
and any fixed
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}
. Thus, taking the spremum of
z
is also convex. Also noting that
\|\alpha\|_{*}^{p}/\eta^{p-1}
is a convex function in
\eta\geqslant 0
. Therefore, we conclude that (
37
) is a convex optimization problem. This completes the proof. ∎

Proof of Proposition
11
.

Applying Theorem
29
, we find that problem (
15
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta(p,\varepsilon)}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\inf_{\eta\geqslant 0}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\}+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}}{\eta^{q-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta^{)}\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\inf_{\eta\geqslant 0}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\}+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}}{\eta^{q-1}}.

One can verify that the objective function of the above optimization problem is linear in
(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}
and convex in
\eta\geqslant 0
, and that
\mathcal{V}_{+}
is a convex compact set. Applying min-max theorem in
Sion 1958
, we have

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\inf_{\eta\geqslant 0}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\}+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}}{\eta^{q-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},\eta\geqslant 0}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\}+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}}{\eta^{q-1}}.

Let
f(p,\delta)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\}+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}}{\eta^{q-1}}
. Similar to the proof of Proposition
10
, using Fenchel duality, we obtain the desired result. ∎

EC.2.2.2. Proofs of Section
3.2

In the following, we give a minimax lemma which will be used in the proof of propositions in Section
3.2
.

Lemma 30
.

Let
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}^{q}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
and
q\in[1,\infty)
,
\delta\geqslant 0
, and
\mathbb{Q}_{0}
be a distribution on
\mathcal{Y}
. We have the following results.

(i)

Assume the loss function
\ell(z,t):\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is the form
\ell(z,t)=\sum_{q_{1}\in\mathcal{I}}C_{q_{1}}\left(\bar{\ell}_{q_{1}}(z,t)\right)^{q_{1}}
, with
\mathcal{I}\subset[1,q]
and
C_{q_{1}}\geqslant 0
, where
\bar{\ell}_{q_{1}}(z,t)
is nonnegative on
\mathbb{R}^{k+1}
, convex and level bounded in
t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
, and Lipschitz continuous in
z
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
with a uniform Lipschitz constant
\operatorname{Lip}(\bar{\ell}_{q_{1}})
, for
q_{1}\in[1,q]
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]=\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right].

(ii)

Let
k=1
. Assume the loss function
\ell(z,t):\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is nonnegative on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, convex in
t\in\mathbb{R}
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
, and Lipschitz continuous in
z
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
with a uniform Lipschitz constant
\operatorname{Lip}({\ell})
. Moreover, assume
t+\ell(z,t)
is level bounded in
t\in\mathbb{R}
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{t+\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell^{q}(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right)^{1/q}\right\}=\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)}\left\{t+\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell^{q}(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right)^{1/q}\right\}.

Proof of Lemma
30
.

(i)

Denoted by
\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t\right)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]
. First, we show three facts below. (a)
\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t\right)
is concave in
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
; (b)
\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t\right)
is convex in
t
for all
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}
; (c)
\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t\right)
is level bounded in
t
for all
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}
. The fact (a) is trivial. Note that
\ell
is nonnegative on
\mathbb{R}^{k+1}
and convex in
t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
. For any distribution
\mathbb{Q|}_{Y}
on
\mathcal{Y}
,
\lambda\in[0,1]
and
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,\lambda t_{1}+(1-\lambda)t_{2})\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\lambda\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t_{1})+(1-\lambda)\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t_{2})\right]

\displaystyle=\lambda\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t_{1}\right]+(1-\lambda)\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t_{2})\right],

where the inequality from the convexity of
\ell
with respect to
t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
. This implies (b). We claim that
\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t\right)
is level bounded in
t
for all
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}
. Otherwise, for fixed
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}
, there exists
A\in\mathbb{R}
such that
S_{A}:=\{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}:\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t\right)\leqslant A\}
is unbounded. Then for each integer
n\geqslant 1
, there exist
t_{n}\in S_{A}
satisfies that
\|t_{n}\|\geqslant n
for arbitrary norm in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
. For any
q_{1}\in\mathcal{I}
, because
\bar{\ell}_{q_{1}}(z,t)
is level bounded for any
z\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\bar{\ell}_{q_{1}}(z,t_{n})\rightarrow\infty
when
n\rightarrow\infty
. In fact, if there exists
M\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\bar{\ell}_{q_{1}}(z,t_{n})\leqslant M
for any
n
, one can verify that the set
\{t:\bar{\ell}_{q_{1}}(z,t)\leqslant M\}
is unbounded, which is contradictory to that
\bar{\ell}_{q_{1}}(z,t)
is level bounded in
t
for any
z
. And then
\bar{\ell}_{q_{1}}(z,t_{n})\rightarrow\infty
when
n\rightarrow\infty
, implies that
\ell(z,t_{n})\rightarrow\infty
when
n\rightarrow\infty
for any
z\in\mathbb{R}
due to the form of
\ell
. By nonnegativity of
\ell
, using Fatou lemma. we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t_{n}\right)=\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t_{n})\right]\geqslant\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t_{n})\right]=\infty.

This is contradictory to that
\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t_{n}\right)\leqslant A
. Hence, we conclude the proof of (c). By (b), we find that
\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t\right)
is lower semicontinuous in
t
. Combining this fact with (c), and applying Weierstrass theorem, there exists a level set
S_{A_{0}}
of
\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t\right)
such that all minimizers of the problem
\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\pi^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t)
are contained in it. The set
S_{A_{0}}
is closed and bounded because that
\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t\right)
is lower semicontinuous and level bounded, and thus,
S_{A_{0}}
is a compact set. Then, the set of all minimizers of the problem
\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\pi^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t)
is a subset of a compact set. Denote by
t(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}):=\arg\inf_{\|t\|}\{\arg\min_{t}\pi^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t)\}
. We will show that
\left\{t(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}):\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)\right\}
is a subset of a compact set. For any
\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)
and
t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
, let
Y\sim\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}
and
Y_{0}\sim\mathbb{Q}_{0}
, and
q_{j}\in\mathcal{I}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathbb{E}\left[C_{q_{j}}\left(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right)^{q_{j}}\right]\right)^{1/{q_{j}}}-\left(\mathbb{E}\left[C_{q_{j}}\left(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(Y_{0}^{\top}\alpha,t)\right)^{q_{j}}\right]\right)^{1/{q_{j}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C_{q_{j}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)-\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(Y_{0}^{\top}\alpha,t)\right|^{q_{j}}\right]\right)^{1/{q_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C_{q_{j}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\operatorname{Lip}(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}})\left|(Y-Y_{0})^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q_{j}}\right]\right)^{1/{q_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C_{q_{j}}\operatorname{Lip}(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}})\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left|(Y-Y_{0})^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q}\right]\right)^{1/{q}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C_{q_{j}}\operatorname{Lip}(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}})\|\alpha\|_{*}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|Y-Y_{0}\right\|^{q}\right]\right)^{1/q}\leqslant C_{q_{j}}\operatorname{Lip}(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}})\|\alpha\|_{*}\delta^{1/q},

(39)

where the first, third and fourth inequalities from the triangle inequality, Jensen’s inequality and Ḧolder inequality, respectively, and we use the definition of OT ball in the last inequality. From (
(i)
), we have that

\displaystyle\pi^{1}_{\ell}\left(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)\right)\leqslant\sum_{j=1,2}\left(\left(\mathbb{E}\left[C_{q_{j}}\left(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(Y_{0}^{\top}\alpha,t\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right))\right)^{q_{j}}\right]\right)^{1/{q_{j}}}+C_{q_{j}}\operatorname{Lip}(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}})\|\alpha\|_{*}\delta^{1/q}\right)^{{q_{j}}}.

(40)

Note that
\left(\mathbb{E}\left[C_{q_{j}}\left(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(Y_{0}^{\top}\alpha,t)\right)^{q_{j}}\right]\right)^{1/{q_{j}}}\rightarrow\infty
as
\|t\|\rightarrow\infty
for
q_{j}\in\mathcal{I}
due to that
\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(z,t)
is level bounded. There exists
\Delta>0
such that

\left(\mathbb{E}\left[C_{q_{j}}\left(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(Y_{0}^{\top}\alpha,t)\right)^{q_{j}}\right]\right)^{1/{q_{j}}}\geqslant C_{q_{j}}\operatorname{Lip}(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}})\|\alpha\|_{*}\delta^{1/q},

and

\left(\mathbb{E}\left[C_{q_{j}}\left(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(Y_{0}^{\top}\alpha,t)\right)^{q_{j}}\right]\right)^{1/{q_{j}}}>\left(\mathbb{E}\left[C_{q_{j}}\left(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(Y_{0}^{\top}\alpha,t\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right))\right)^{q_{j}}\right]\right)^{1/{q_{j}}}+2C_{q_{j}}\operatorname{Lip}(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}})\|\alpha\|_{*}\delta^{1/q},

for all
t\notin\{t:\|t-t(\mathbb{Q}_{0})\|\leqslant\Delta\}
and
q_{j}\in\mathcal{I}
. This, combined with (
(i)
), imply that

\displaystyle\pi^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t)\geqslant

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1,2}\left(\left(\mathbb{E}\left[C_{q_{j}}\left(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(Y_{0}^{\top}\alpha,t)\right)^{q_{j}}\right]\right)^{1/{q_{j}}}-C_{q_{j}}\operatorname{Lip}(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}})\|\alpha\|_{*}\delta^{1/q}\right)^{{q_{j}}}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1,2}\left(\left(\mathbb{E}\left[C_{q_{j}}\left(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}}(Y_{0}^{\top}\alpha,t\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right))\right)^{q_{j}}\right]\right)^{1/{q_{j}}}+C_{q_{j}}\operatorname{Lip}(\bar{\ell}_{q_{j}})\|\alpha\|_{*}\delta^{1/q}\right)^{{q_{j}}},

(41)

for all
t\notin\{t:\|t-t(\mathbb{Q}_{0})\|\leqslant\Delta\}
. Applying (
40
) and (
(i)
), we have
\left\{t(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}):\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)\right\}\subseteq\{t:\|t-t(\mathbb{Q}_{0})\|\leqslant\Delta\}
. Using the minimax theorem in
Sion 1958
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\pi^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t)

\displaystyle=\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)}\inf_{t\in\{t:\|t-t(\mathbb{Q}_{0})\|\leqslant\Delta\}}\pi^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t)

\displaystyle=\inf_{t\in\{t:\|t-t(\mathbb{Q}_{0})\|\leqslant\Delta\}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)}\pi^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t)\geqslant\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\mathbb{Q}_{0}\right)}\pi^{1}_{\ell}(\mathbb{Q}|_{Y},t).

The converse direction is trivial. Hence, we complete the proof of (i).

(ii)

The proof is similar to (i).

∎

Proof of Proposition
13
.

(i)

Let
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}
,
\forall y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
. Applying Lemma
30
, equation (
26
) and minimax theorem, problem (
13
) with (
14
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\rho^{(1)}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left(Y^{\top}\alpha\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]+\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\delta||\alpha||_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0}\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]+\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\delta||\alpha||_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]+\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\delta||\alpha||_{*}.

Applying the Fenchel duality, we have the desired result.

(ii)

Applying Corollary 1 of
Wu et al. 2022
and minimax theorem, we find that problem (
13
) with (
14
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\rho^{(1)}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left(Y^{\top}\alpha\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0}\left(\left(\rho^{(1)}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left(Y^{\top}\alpha\right)\right)^{1/q}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}||\alpha||_{*}\right)^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left(\left(\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right)^{1/q}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}||\alpha||_{*}\right)^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\left(\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right)^{1/q}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}||\alpha||_{*}\right)^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\left(\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right)^{1/q}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}||\alpha||_{*}\right)^{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right)^{1/q}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}||\alpha||_{*}\right)^{q}.

Thus we only need to consider the inner problem

\displaystyle\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right)^{1/q}+C\delta^{\frac{1}{q}}||\alpha||_{*},

for
q\in(1,\infty)
.
Similar to the proof of Proposition
10
, we can apply the Fenchel duality theory to the above problem and then obtain the desired result.

∎

Proof of Proposition
14
.

Applying Lemma
30
, Theorem
29
and minimax theorem in
Sion 1958
, problem (
13
) with (
14
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\rho^{(1)}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left(Y^{\top}\alpha\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k}}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}^{k},\eta\geqslant 0}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-(\ell(\cdot,t))^{*}(z)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\}+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}}{\eta^{q-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R}^{k},\eta\geqslant 0}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-(\ell(\cdot,t))^{*}(z)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\}+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}}{\eta^{q-1}}.

Applying the Fenchel duality, we have the desired result. ∎

Proof of Example
3.4
.

The robustified conditional mean-variance portfolio allocation problem in
Nguyen et al. 2024
is as follows:

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbb{Q}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}})\\
\mathbb{Q}\left(X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0})\right)\in\mathcal{N}_{\omega}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{Variance}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[Y^{\top}\alpha\mid X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)\right]-\theta\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[Y^{\top}\alpha\mid X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)\right],

where
\mathcal{N}_{\omega}=[\varepsilon_{0},1]
,
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1]
and
\theta\geqslant 0
. Suppose in addition that
\mathcal{X}=\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}},\mathcal{Y}=\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}},\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\widehat{x})=\|x-\widehat{x}\|^{2}
and
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y,\widehat{y})=\|y-\widehat{y}\|_{2}^{2}
. By Theorem
3
and the minimization representation of variance, one can verify that the problem (
19
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in{\cal V}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\operatorname{Variance}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[Y^{\top}\alpha\right]-\theta\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[Y^{\top}\alpha\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in{\cal V}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\left(Y^{\top}\alpha-t\right)^{2}-\theta\cdot Y^{\top}\alpha\right]

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in{\cal V}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right],

where
\ell(z,t)=\left(z-t\right)^{2}-\theta z
. By Lemma
30
, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]=\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right].

Due to the above equation, we can reformulate problem (
19
) as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in{\cal V}}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right].

Applying Theorem
29
and min-max theorem, the problem (
19
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in{\cal V}}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in{\cal V},\delta\geqslant 0}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R},\eta\geqslant 0}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z,t)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\}+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{q}}{\eta^{q-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R},\eta\geqslant 0}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z,t)+\eta\cdot\frac{z^{2}}{4}\right\}+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{2}}{\eta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R},\eta\geqslant 0}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z,t)+\eta\cdot\frac{z^{2}}{4}\right\}+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{2}}{\eta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R},1\geqslant\eta\geqslant 0}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\frac{\left(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\theta/2-t\right)^{2}}{1-\eta}+\delta\frac{\|\alpha\|_{*}^{2}}{\eta}-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4}-t\theta.

Applying the Fenchel duality, the problem (
19
) can be reformulated as an infimum problem

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},0\leqslant\eta\leqslant 1,t\in\mathbb{R},v}~\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4}-t\theta

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~v_{i}\geqslant\frac{\left(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\theta/2-t\right)^{2}}{1-\eta},~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~v_{N+1}\geqslant||\alpha||_{*}^{2}/\eta,

where
\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})
, the conjugate function of
\sigma(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})
, is given in Example
3.2
. Thus, substituting the
\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})
in Example
3.2
, the robustified conditional mean-variance portfolio allocation problem (
19
), proposed in
Nguyen et al. 2024
, can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},0\leqslant\eta\leqslant 1,t\in\mathbb{R},z,s}~z^{\top}b-\frac{\theta^{2}}{4}-t\theta

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~z\geqslant 0,~A^{\top}z\geqslant s

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~s_{i}\geqslant\frac{\left(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\theta/2-t\right)^{2}}{1-\eta},~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~s_{N+1}\geqslant||\alpha||_{*}^{2}/\eta,~s_{N+2}\geqslant 0,

where
A\in\mathbb{R}^{(N+6)\times(N+2)}
,and
b\in\mathbb{R}^{N+6}
are defined by

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}1&\dots&1&0&0\\
-1&\dots&-1&0&0\\
0&\dots&0&0&1\\
0&\dots&0&0&-1\\
1&\dots&0&0&-\frac{1}{N}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&\dots&1&0&-\frac{1}{N}\\
-a_{1}&\dots&-a_{N}&-a_{N+1}&-a_{N+2}\\
a_{1}&\dots&a_{N}&a_{N+1}&a_{N+2}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{ with }a=\begin{pmatrix}-d_{1}\\
\vdots\\
-d_{m}\\
d_{m+1}\\
\vdots\\
d_{N}\\
-1\\[2.15277pt]
\displaystyle\delta_{0}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}d_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{ and }b=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
-1\\[3.0pt]
\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon_{0}}\\
-1\\[3.0pt]
0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
17
.

The proof is similar to Proposition
13
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
18
.

The problem problem (
13
) with (
14
) with
\rho=\rho^{(2)}
can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\rho^{(2)}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left(Y^{\top}\alpha\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}~\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}t+\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell^{q}(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}~t+\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell^{q}(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0}~t+\left(\sup_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}|_{Y}}\left[\ell^{q}(Y^{\top}\alpha,t)\right]\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}~t+\left(\inf_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\ell^{q}(z,t)-\lambda\left|z-\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q}\right\}+\lambda\delta\left\|\alpha\right\|_{*}^{q}\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R}}~t+\left(\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\inf_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\ell^{q}(z,t)-\lambda\left|z-\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q}\right\}+\lambda\delta\left\|\alpha\right\|_{*}^{q}\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R}}~t+\left(\inf_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\ell^{q}(z,t)-\lambda\left|z-\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q}\right\}+\lambda\delta\left\|\alpha\right\|_{*}^{q}\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R},\lambda\geqslant 0}~t+\left(\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\ell^{q}(z,t)-\lambda\left|z-\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q}\right\}+\lambda\delta\left\|\alpha\right\|_{*}^{q}\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R},\lambda\geqslant 0}~t+\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\ell^{q}(z,t)-\lambda\left|z-\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q}\right\}+\lambda\delta\left\|\alpha\right\|_{*}^{q}\right)^{1/q},

where the first equality is from Lemma EC.8 of
Wu et al. 2022
, the second and sixth equalities are from minimax theorem in
Sion 1958
, the fourth equality is from Theorem
29
, and other equalities hold for the reason that
x^{1/q}
is a nonnegative and increasing function and thus it does not change optimal solution of the optimization problem. Using the Fenchel duality, we can reformulate the above problem as

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R},\lambda\geqslant 0}~t+\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\ell^{q}(z,t)-\lambda\left|z-\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q}\right\}+\lambda\delta\left\|\alpha\right\|_{*}^{q}\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R},\lambda\geqslant 0,v,y}t+\sigma^{*}\left(v|\mathcal{V}_{+}\right)+C_{q}^{1}y^{\frac{1}{1-q}}
