\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~v_{i}\geqslant y\cdot\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\ell^{q}(z,t)-\lambda\left|z-\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q}\right\},i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~v_{N+1}\geqslant\lambda y\left\|\alpha\right\|_{*}^{q},y\geqslant 0.

Let
u=\lambda y
and
w=y^{\frac{1}{1-q}}
. By the convexity of
\ell
in
z
, one can verify that
\frac{\ell^{q}(z,t)}{w^{q-1}}
is convex in
z
, and thus
\left(\frac{\ell^{q}(\cdot,t)}{w^{q-1}}\right)^{**}=\frac{\ell^{q}(\cdot,t)}{w^{q-1}}
. Moreover, we find that
\frac{\ell^{q}(z,t)}{w^{q-1}}
is jointly convex in
(z,t,w)
and thus
\left(\frac{\ell^{q}(\cdot,t)}{w^{q-1}}\right)^{*}
is jointly concave in
(t,w)
according to the definition of concave conjugate function. Let
u=\eta^{1-q}
. Similar to the proof of Theorem
29
, the problem can be further reformulated as

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R},u\geqslant 0,v,y}t+\sigma^{*}\left(v|\mathcal{V}_{+}\right)+C_{q}^{1}y^{\frac{1}{1-q}}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~v_{i}\geqslant\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{y\ell^{q}(z,t)-u\left|z-\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q}\right\},i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~v_{N+1}\geqslant u\left\|\alpha\right\|_{*}^{q},y\geqslant 0,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R},u\geqslant 0,v,w}t+\sigma^{*}\left(v|\mathcal{V}_{+}\right)+C_{q}^{1}w

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~v_{i}\geqslant\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\frac{\ell^{q}(z,t)}{w^{q-1}}-u\left|z-\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right|^{q}\right\},i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~v_{N+1}\geqslant u\left\|\alpha\right\|_{*}^{q},w\geqslant 0,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},\eta\geqslant 0,t\in\mathbb{R},v,w\geqslant 0}~t+\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})+C_{q}^{1}w

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~v_{i}\geqslant\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{x\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\left(\frac{\ell^{q}(\cdot,t)}{w^{q-1}}\right)^{*}(x)+\eta\cdot\frac{|x|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\},~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~v_{N+1}\geqslant||\alpha||_{*}^{q}/\eta^{q-1}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
19
.

Let
\mathcal{P}=\bigcup\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)
be a convex set. We first prove that

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}=

\displaystyle\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\kappa

(42)

\displaystyle~~\text{s.t.}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l.

If
\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l
for any
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}
. Then

\left\{\kappa:\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}\subseteq\left\{\kappa:\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}\leqslant\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\},~\forall{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P},

and

\displaystyle\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}\leqslant\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}.

(43)

Let
\kappa^{*}=\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}
. We prove the equality (
42
) in the following three cases: (i)
\kappa^{*}=\infty
; (ii)
\kappa^{*}=-\infty
; (iii)
\kappa^{*}
is finite.

(i)

From the inequality (
43
), we directly obtain

\infty\geqslant\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}\geqslant\kappa^{*}=\infty.

Thus, the equality (
42
) holds.

(ii)

If
\kappa^{*}=-\infty
, for any
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l
for any
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
. Then for any
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l
for any
{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}
. Thus, for any
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l
. Hence,

\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}=-\infty=\kappa^{*}.

(iii)

Note that

\kappa^{*}\geqslant\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}~\forall~{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}.

Thus,

\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa^{*})\right]\leqslant l,~\forall~{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P},

which implies that

\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa^{*})\right]\leqslant l.

Then
\kappa^{*}\in\left\{\kappa:\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}
, and thus

\kappa^{*}\geqslant\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}.

Combining with inequality (
43
), we obtain the equality (
42
).

Combining the above three cases, we obtain the equality (
42
) in all instances. Note that similar reasoning also appears in Proposition 1 of
Guo and Xu 2019
, where the finiteness of

\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}

is assumed. Here, we establish (
42
) in the general case to ensure completeness.

Next, we apply the equality (
42
) to derive the desired result. In particular, for
\rho=\rho^{(3)}
, the problem (
17
) becomes

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\bigcup\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\rho_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[Y^{\top}\alpha\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\bigcup\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\inf\left\{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\kappa

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\bigcup\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l.

And then using the equation (
26
), we have

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\kappa

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\bigcup\limits_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\kappa

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sup_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}\right)}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbb{Q}}|_{Y}}\left[u(-Y^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)\right]\leqslant l

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\kappa

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}u(-\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)+\operatorname{Lip}(u)\delta||\alpha||_{*}\leqslant l.

Using the Fenchel duality, we have

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\kappa

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}u(-\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\kappa)+\operatorname{Lip}(u)\delta||\alpha||_{*}\leqslant l

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\inf_{\kappa\in\mathbb{R}}\kappa

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}\inf_{v}\left\{\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})|v-\nu(\alpha,\kappa)\geqslant 0\right\}\leqslant l

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha,\kappa,v}\kappa

\displaystyle~~\text{s.t.}~\alpha\in\mathcal{A},\kappa\in\mathbb{R},

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})\leqslant l,~v-\nu(\alpha,\kappa)\geqslant 0,

where
\nu(\alpha,\kappa)^{\top}=(u(-\widehat{y}_{1}^{\top}\alpha-\kappa),...,u(-\widehat{y}_{N}^{\top}\alpha-\kappa),\operatorname{Lip}(u)||\alpha||_{*})\in\mathbb{R}^{N+1}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
21
.

Similar to the proof of Proposition
19
, applying the equality (
42
), Theorem
29
, min-max theorem, and the Fenchel duality, we have the desired result. ∎

EC.2.2.2. Proofs of Section
3.3

Proof of Proposition
22
.

(i)

For
q=1
and
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}
, applying Theorem 5 in
Wu et al. 2022
, we find that problem (
13
) with (
14
) can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\rho_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}^{h}\left[\ell(Y^{\top}\alpha)\right]+\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\left\|h_{-}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\delta\|{\alpha}\|_{*}.

According to Proposition 10.3 of
Denneberg, D. 2013
, we can reformulate the above inner supremum problem as

\displaystyle\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\sup_{\bar{p}\in\mathcal{V}_{p}^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{p}_{i}\ell\left(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right)+\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\left\|h_{-}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\delta\|{\alpha}\|_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+},\bar{p}\in\mathcal{V}_{p}^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{p}_{i}\ell\left(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right)+\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\left\|h_{-}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\delta\|{\alpha}\|_{*}.

Using the Fenchel duality theory, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+},\bar{p}\in\mathcal{V}_{p}^{h}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{p}_{i}\ell\left(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha\right)+\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\left\|h_{-}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\delta\|{\alpha}\|_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{v}\left\{\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h})|v-\nu(\alpha)\geqslant 0\right\},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h}=\left\{p^{\prime\prime}=\left(p,\delta,\bar{p}\right):(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0,\bar{p}\in\mathcal{V}_{p}^{h}\right\},

\mathcal{V}_{p}^{h}=\left\{\bar{p}:\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{p}_{i}=1,\bar{p}_{i}\geqslant 0,i=1,...,N,h\left(\sum_{i\in J}p_{i}\right)\leqslant\sum_{i\in J}\bar{p}_{i},\forall J\subset\{1,...,N\}\right\},

and

\displaystyle\nu(\alpha)^{\top}=\left(0,...,0,\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)\left\|h_{-}^{\prime}\right\|_{\infty}\|\alpha\|_{*},\ell(\widehat{y}_{1}^{\top}\alpha),...,\ell(\widehat{y}_{N}^{\top}\alpha)\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{2N+1}.

Combing with the outer minimization problem, we obtain the result in (i).

(ii)

Similar to the case (i), applying Theorem 5 in
Wu et al. 2022
, Proposition 10.3 of
Denneberg, D. 2013
, and the Fenchel duality theory, we obtain the result.

∎

Proof of Example
3.5
.

For the full optimal transport model in Section
2.1
, we have

{\cal V}:=\left\{(p,\delta):\exists\varepsilon\text{~such that }(p,\delta,\varepsilon)\in\mathcal{V}_{0}\right\},

where

\displaystyle{\cal V}_{0}:=\Bigg\{(p,\delta,\varepsilon):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{N}_{\omega},p_{i}\in\left[0,\frac{\varepsilon}{N}\right],i=1,...,N,\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}=1,

\displaystyle\delta=\varepsilon\left(\delta_{0}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}d_{i}\right)-\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}+\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}p_{i}d_{i}\Bigg\}.

Let

{\cal V}_{+}:=\left\{(p,\delta):(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0\right\},

and

\displaystyle{\cal V}_{+}^{h}:=\left\{(p,\delta,\bar{p}):(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+},\bar{p}\in\mathcal{V}_{p}^{h}\right\}.

Then in the following we obtain the conjugate function of the indicator function of
\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h}
to have the tractable reformulation for the full optimal transport model. The conjugate function of the indicator function of
\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h}
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}(v\mid\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h})=\sup_{(p,\delta,\bar{p})\in\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h}}v^{\top}(p,\delta,\bar{p})=\sup_{(p,\delta,\varepsilon)\in\mathcal{V}_{0},\delta\geqslant 0,\bar{p}\in\mathcal{V}_{p}^{h}}v^{\top}(p,\delta,\bar{p})+0\cdot\varepsilon.

Denote
p^{\prime\prime}=(p,\delta,\bar{p},\varepsilon)
.
One can verify that the above problem is a convex optimization problem, and thus we can apply strong duality theory. The Lagrangian function is

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(p^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(v,0)^{\top}p^{\prime\prime}+\lambda\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}-1\right)+\mu\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{p}_{i}-1\right)-\sum_{J\subset[N]}\zeta_{J}\left(h\left(\sum_{i\in J}p_{i}\right)-\sum_{i\in J}\bar{p}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i}^{1}\left(-p_{i}\right)-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\gamma_{i}\left(-\bar{p}_{i}\right)-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i}^{2}\left(p_{i}-\frac{\varepsilon}{N}\right)-\eta_{1}(\frac{1}{R_{\mathcal{N}_{\omega}}}-\varepsilon)-\eta_{2}\left(\varepsilon-\frac{1}{L_{\mathcal{N}_{\omega}}}\right)

\displaystyle-\xi_{1}(-\delta)+\xi_{2}\left(\delta-\varepsilon(\delta_{0}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}d_{i})+\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}-\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}p_{i}d_{i}\right),

where
p^{\top}=(p_{1},...,p_{N})\in[0,1]^{N}
,
q^{\top}=(q_{1},...,q_{N})\in[0,1]^{N}
,
\lambda,\mu,\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\zeta_{J},\tau_{i}^{1},\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}^{2},\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\xi_{1}\geqslant 0
. Then we simplify the problem
\sup_{p^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{L}(p^{\prime\prime})
. One can verify that
\sup_{p^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{L}(p^{\prime\prime})
can be decomposed into four optimization problems, which are only related to
\varepsilon
,
p
,
\bar{p}
, and
\delta
, respectively. For the problem related to
\varepsilon
with constant terms, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\geqslant 0}\Bigg\{\varepsilon\left[-\xi_{2}(\delta_{0}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}d_{i})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i}^{2}+\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\right]-\frac{\eta_{1}}{R_{\mathcal{N}_{\omega}}}+\frac{\eta_{2}}{L_{\mathcal{N}_{\omega}}}-\lambda-\mu\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}-\frac{\eta_{1}}{R_{\mathcal{N}_{\omega}}}+\frac{\eta_{2}}{L_{\mathcal{N}_{\omega}}}-\lambda-\mu\\
~~~~~~~~~\text{ if }-\xi_{2}(\delta_{0}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}d_{i})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i}^{2}+\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\leqslant 0\\
\infty~~~~~\text{ else. }\end{cases}

Let

c_{i}=\begin{cases}\xi_{2}d_{i},&i=1,\ldots,m\\
-\xi_{2}d_{i},&i=m+1,\ldots,N.\end{cases}

For the problem related to
p
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{p\geqslant 0}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\left[v_{i}+\lambda+\tau_{i}^{1}-\tau_{i}^{2}+c_{i}\right]-\sum_{J\subset\{1,...,N\}}\zeta_{J}h\left(\sum_{i\in J}p_{i}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{p\geqslant 0,w_{1},...,w_{2^{N}-2}\geqslant 0}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\left[v_{i}+\lambda+\tau_{i}^{1}-\tau_{i}^{2}+c_{i}\right]-\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\zeta_{j}h(w_{j})|w_{j}=\sum_{k\in I_{j}}p_{k}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\phi_{1},...,\phi_{2^{N}-2}}\sup_{p\geqslant 0,w_{1},...,w_{2^{N}-2}\geqslant 0}\Bigg\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\left[v_{i}+\lambda+\tau_{i}^{1}-\tau_{i}^{2}+c_{i}\right]

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~-\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\zeta_{j}h(w_{j})-\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\phi_{j}(w_{j}-\sum_{k\in I_{j}}p_{k})\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\phi_{1},...,\phi_{2^{N}-2}}\Bigg\{\sup_{p\geqslant 0}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\left[v_{i}+\lambda+\tau_{i}^{1}-\tau_{i}^{2}+c_{i}\right]+\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\phi_{j}\sum_{k\in I_{j}}p_{k}\right\}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~+\sup_{w_{1},...,w_{2^{N}-2}\geqslant 0}\left\{-\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\zeta_{j}h(w_{j})-\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\phi_{j}w_{j}\right\}\Bigg\}.

We find that

\displaystyle\sup_{p\geqslant 0}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\left[v_{i}+\lambda+\tau_{i}^{1}-\tau_{i}^{2}+c_{i}\right]+\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\phi_{j}\sum_{k\in I_{j}}p_{k}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{p\geqslant 0}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\left[v_{i}+\lambda+\tau_{i}^{1}-\tau_{i}^{2}+c_{i}+\sum_{j:i\in I_{j}}\phi_{j}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }v_{i}+\lambda+\tau_{i}^{1}-\tau_{i}^{2}+c_{i}+\sum_{j:i\in I_{j}}\phi_{j}\leqslant 0,i=1,...,N\\
\infty&\text{ else, }\end{cases}

and that

\displaystyle\sup_{w_{1},...,w_{2^{N}-2}\geqslant 0}\left\{-\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\zeta_{j}h(w_{j})-\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\phi_{j}w_{j}\right\}=\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\zeta_{j}h^{*}(\frac{-\phi_{j}}{\zeta_{j}}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{p\geqslant 0}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\left[v_{i}+\lambda+\tau_{i}^{1}-\tau_{i}^{2}+c_{i}\right]-\sum_{J\subset\{1,...,N\}}\zeta_{J}h(\sum_{i\in J}p_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{\phi_{1},...,\phi_{2^{N}-2}}\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\zeta_{j}h^{*}(\frac{-\phi_{j}}{\zeta_{j}})

\displaystyle~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~v_{i}+\lambda+\tau_{i}^{1}-\tau_{i}^{2}+c_{i}+\sum_{j:i\in I_{j}}\phi_{j}\leqslant 0,~i=1,...,N.

For the problem related to
\bar{p}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\bar{p}\geqslant 0}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{p}_{i}\left[v_{N+1+i}+\mu+{\sum_{j:i\in I_{j}}\zeta_{j}}+\gamma_{i}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }v_{N+1+i}+\mu+{\sum_{j:i\in I_{j}}\zeta_{j}}+\gamma_{i}\leqslant 0,~i=1,...,N,\\
\infty&\text{ else. }\end{cases}

For the problem related to
\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\delta\geqslant 0}\left\{\delta^{*}\left[v_{N+1}+\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}\right]\right\}=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if }v_{N+1}+\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}\leqslant 0,\\
\infty&\text{ else. }\end{cases}

Then the conjugate function of
\sigma((p,\delta,\bar{p})|\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h})
,
\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h})
, is equivalent to

\displaystyle\inf-\frac{\eta_{1}}{R_{\mathcal{N}_{\omega}}}+\frac{\eta_{2}}{L_{\mathcal{N}_{\omega}}}-\lambda-\mu+\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\zeta_{j}h^{*}(\frac{-\phi_{j}}{\zeta_{j}})

\displaystyle~\text{s.t.}~\lambda,\mu,\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{R},\left\{\phi_{j}\right\}_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\in\mathbb{R}^{2^{N}-2},\left\{\zeta_{j}\right\}_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2},\left\{\tau_{i}^{1}\right\}_{i=1}^{N},\left\{\tau_{i}^{2}\right\}_{i=1}^{N},\left\{\gamma_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{N},\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\xi_{1}\geqslant 0

\displaystyle~~~~~~-\xi_{2}(\delta_{0}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}d_{i})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i}^{2}+\eta_{1}-\eta_{2}\leqslant 0,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~v_{i}+\lambda+\tau_{i}^{1}-\tau_{i}^{2}+c_{i}+\sum_{j:i\in I_{j}}\phi_{j}\leqslant 0,~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~c_{i}=\xi_{2}d_{i},i=1,\ldots,m,~c_{i}=-\xi_{2}d_{i},i=m+1,\ldots,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~v_{N+1+i}+\mu+{\sum_{j:i\in I_{j}}\zeta_{j}}+\gamma_{i}\leqslant 0,~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~v_{N+1}+\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}\leqslant 0.

∎

Proof of Example
3.6
.

For the partial optimal transport model in Section
2.1
, we have

{\cal V}:=\left\{(p,\delta):{\varepsilon}=\min_{\beta\in\mathcal{N}_{\omega}}\beta,p_{i}\in[0,\frac{1}{N\varepsilon}],i=1,...,N,\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}=1,\delta=\delta_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}p_{i}d_{i}\right\}.

One can verify that
\varepsilon
is a constant given
\mathcal{N}_{\omega}
. Then

\mathcal{V}_{+}:=\left\{(p,\delta):(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V},\delta\geqslant 0\right\},

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h}:=\left\{p^{\prime\prime}=(p,\delta,\bar{p}):(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+},\bar{p}\in\mathcal{V}_{p}^{h}\right\},

and we want to obtain the conjugate function of the indicator function of
\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h}
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{*}(v\mid\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h})=\sup_{p^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h}}v^{\top}p^{\prime\prime}.

One can verify that the above problem is a convex optimization problem, and thus we can apply strong duality theory. Let

c_{i}^{\prime}=\begin{cases}\xi_{2}d_{i},&i=1,\ldots,m\\
0,&i=m+1,\ldots,N.\end{cases}

Similar to the proof of Example
3.5
, the conjugate function of
\sigma((p,\delta,\bar{p})|\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h})
,
\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+}^{h})
, is equivalent to

\displaystyle\inf-\lambda-\mu+\frac{1}{N\varepsilon}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\tau_{i}^{2}-\xi_{2}\delta_{0}+\sum_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\zeta_{j}h^{*}(\frac{-\phi_{j}}{\zeta_{j}})

\displaystyle~\text{s.t.}~\alpha\in\mathcal{A},~\lambda,\mu,\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{R},\left\{\phi_{j}\right\}_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2}\in\mathbb{R}^{2^{N}-2},\left\{\zeta_{j}\right\}_{j=1}^{2^{N}-2},\left\{\tau_{i}^{1}\right\}_{i=1}^{N},\left\{\tau_{i}^{2}\right\}_{i=1}^{N},\left\{\gamma_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{N},\xi_{1}\geqslant 0,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~v_{i}+\lambda+\tau_{i}^{1}-\tau_{i}^{2}+c_{i}^{\prime}+\sum_{j:i\in I_{j}}\phi_{j}\leqslant 0,~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~c_{i}^{\prime}=\xi_{2}d_{i},i=1,\ldots,m,~c_{i}=0,i=m+1,\ldots,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~v_{N+1+i}+\mu+{\sum_{j:i\in I_{j}}\zeta_{j}}+\gamma_{i}\leqslant 0,~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~v_{N+1}+\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}\leqslant 0.

∎

Proof of Example
3.7
.

The robustified conditional mean-
\mathrm{CVaR}
portfolio allocation problem in
Nguyen et al. 2024
is as follows:

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbb{Q}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}})\\
\mathbb{Q}\left(X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0})\right)\in\mathcal{N}_{\omega}\end{subarray}}\operatorname{CVaR}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{1-\kappa}\left[Y^{\top}\alpha\mid X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)\right]-\theta\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[Y^{\top}\alpha\mid X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)\right],

where
\mathcal{N}_{\omega}=[\varepsilon_{0},1]
,
\varepsilon_{0}\in(0,1]
,
\theta\geqslant 0
,
\kappa\in[0,1]
,

\operatorname{CVaR}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{1-\kappa}(Z)=\frac{1}{\kappa}\int_{1-\kappa}^{1}\operatorname{VaR}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\phi}(-Z)\operatorname{d}\phi,

and
\operatorname{VaR}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\phi}(Z)=\inf\left\{z\in\mathbb{R}:\mathbb{Q}(Z\leqslant z)\geqslant\phi\right\}
. Suppose in addition that
\mathcal{X}=\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}},\mathcal{Y}=\mathbb{R}^{n_{2}},\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(x,\widehat{x})=\|x-\widehat{x}\|^{2}
and
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y,\widehat{y})=\|y-\widehat{y}\|_{2}^{2}
. One can verify that the objective function can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\operatorname{CVaR}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{1-\kappa}\left[Y^{\top}\alpha\mid X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)\right]-\theta\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[Y^{\top}\alpha\mid X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1+\theta)\rho_{\mathbb{Q}}^{h}[-Y^{\top}\alpha\mid X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)],

where

\displaystyle h(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{\theta}{1+\theta}x&x\in[0,1-\kappa],\\
\frac{1+\theta\kappa}{\kappa(1+\theta)}x-\frac{1-\kappa}{\kappa(1+\theta)}&x\in(1-\kappa,1].\end{cases}

Applying Theorem 5 in
Wu et al. 2022
, we have

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbb{Q}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}})\\
\mathbb{Q}\left(X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0})\right)\in\mathcal{N}_{\omega}\end{subarray}}(1+\theta)\rho_{\mathbb{Q}}^{h}\left[-Y^{\top}\alpha\mid X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}(1+\theta)\left[\rho_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}^{h}\left(-Y^{\top}\alpha\right)+\left\|h_{-}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\delta(p,\varepsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\alpha}\|_{2}\right],

where
\left\|h_{-}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}=\left(1+\frac{1-\kappa}{\kappa(1+\theta)^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Using the minimization form of CVaR and minimax theorem, we reformulate the inner problem of the above problem as

\displaystyle\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}(1+\theta)\left[\rho_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}^{h}\left(-Y^{\top}\alpha\right)+\left\|h_{-}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\alpha}\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[t+\frac{1}{\kappa}(-Y^{\top}\alpha-t)_{+}-\theta\cdot Y^{\top}\alpha\right]+(1+\theta)\left\|h_{-}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\alpha\|_{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\sup_{(p,\delta)\in\mathcal{V}_{+}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}\mathrm{I}_{\widehat{y}_{i}}}\left[t+\frac{1}{\kappa}(-Y^{\top}\alpha-t)_{+}-\theta\cdot Y^{\top}\alpha\right]+(1+\theta)\left\|h_{-}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\alpha\|_{2}\right\}.

Applying the Fenchel duality theory, the problem (
22
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R},v}~\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})+C_{\theta,\kappa}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~v_{i}\geqslant\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha,t),i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~v_{N+1}\geqslant 0,

where
C_{\theta,\kappa}=\frac{1}{4}\left(\left(1+\theta\right)^{2}+\frac{1-\kappa}{\kappa}\right)
,
\ell(y^{\top}\alpha,t)=t+\frac{1}{\kappa}(-y^{\top}\alpha-t)_{+}-\theta\cdot y^{\top}\alpha
, and
\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})
, the conjugate function of
\sigma(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})
, is given in Example
3.2
. Thus, substituting the
\sigma^{*}(v|\mathcal{V}_{+})
in Example
3.2
, the robustified conditional mean-
\mathrm{CVaR}
portfolio allocation problem (
22
), proposed in
Nguyen et al. 2024
, can be reformulated as

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha,t,v,z}~z^{\top}b+C_{\theta,\kappa}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle~~~\text{s.t.}~\alpha\in\mathcal{A},~t\in\mathbb{R},~v-\nu(\alpha,t)\geqslant 0,~A^{\top}z\geqslant v,~z\geqslant 0.

And thus, it is equivalent to

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},t\in\mathbb{R},z\geqslant 0,v_{N+1},s}~z^{\top}b+s

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~v_{N+1}\geqslant 0,~A^{\top}z\geqslant(\ell(\widehat{y}_{1}^{\top}\alpha,t),\dots,\ell(\widehat{y}_{N}^{\top}\alpha,t),v_{N+1},0)^{\top},

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~s\geqslant C_{\theta,\kappa}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{2}^{2},

where
A\in\mathbb{R}^{(N+6)\times(N+2)}
,and
b\in\mathbb{R}^{N+6}
are defined by

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}1&\dots&1&0&0\\
-1&\dots&-1&0&0\\
0&\dots&0&0&1\\
0&\dots&0&0&-1\\
1&\dots&0&0&-\frac{1}{N}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
0&\dots&1&0&-\frac{1}{N}\\
-a_{1}&\dots&-a_{N}&-a_{N+1}&-a_{N+2}\\
a_{1}&\dots&a_{N}&a_{N+1}&a_{N+2}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{ with }a=\begin{pmatrix}-d_{1}\\
\vdots\\
-d_{m}\\
d_{m+1}\\
\vdots\\
d_{N}\\
-1\\[2.15277pt]
\displaystyle\delta_{0}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}d_{i}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{ and }b=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
-1\\[3.0pt]
\tfrac{1}{\varepsilon_{0}}\\
-1\\[3.0pt]
0\\
\vdots\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

∎

EC.3. Full Reformulation for the Conditional Expectation Case Based on Admissible Sets from Theorems 1 and 2, and Comparison with
Nguyen et al. 2024

In this section, we present full reformulations of the conditional expectation problem under our union-ball framework, leveraging the admissible sets characterized in Theorems 1 and 2. These yield tractable convex programs for the full and partial optimal transport models, respectively. While the primary focus is on developing these reformulations, we also include a brief comparison with the formulation proposed in
Nguyen et al. 2024
, highlighting key structural differences in terms of tractability, compactness, and interpretability.

(A)

Note that full optimal transport model (
4
) given by
Nguyen et al. 2024
is equivalent to problem (
15
) with set
\mathcal{V}
given in Theorem
3
. Applying the conjugate of the indicator function of
\mathcal{V}_{+}
in Example
3.2
to Propositions
10
and
11
, we have the following results.

(i)

Let
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
and
\ell:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a convex and Lipschitz continuous function.
The problem (
15
) can be solved by the convex program

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},z\geqslant 0}~z^{\top}b

\displaystyle~~~~\text{s.t.}~\left[A^{\top}z\right]_{i}\geqslant\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha),~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\left[A^{\top}z\right]_{N+1}\geqslant\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)||\alpha||_{*},

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\left[A^{\top}z\right]_{N+2}\geqslant 0.

Note that the objective function of above reformulation is linear. Moreover, given
\alpha
, this formulation has
N+6
variables,
2N+8
linear constraints and
1
convex constraint.

(ii)

Let
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}^{q}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
and
q\in(1,\infty)
.

(1)

Assume the loss function
\ell
takes one of the following two forms, multiplied by
C>0
:

(a)

\ell_{1}(x)=x+b
or
\ell_{1}(x)=-x+b
with some
b\in\mathbb{R}
;

(b)

\ell_{2}(x)=\left|x-b_{1}\right|+b_{2}
with some
b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
.

The problem (
15
) can be solved by the convex program

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},v_{N+1}\geqslant 0,z\geqslant 0,s}~z^{\top}b+s

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~[A^{\top}z]_{i}\geqslant\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha),~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~[A^{\top}z]_{N+1}\geqslant v_{N+1},

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~[A^{\top}z]_{N+2}\geqslant 0,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~s\geqslant C_{q}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{*}^{\frac{q}{q-1}},

where
C_{q}=C^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\left(q^{\frac{1}{1-q}}-q^{\frac{q}{1-q}}\right)
. Note that the objective function of above reformulation is linear. Moreover, given
\alpha
, this formulation has
N+8
variables,
2N+9
linear constraints and
1
convex constraint.

(2)

Assume the loss function
\ell
is of the form
\ell(x)=\left(C\cdot\ell_{i}(x)\right)^{q}
, where
C>0
,
q\in(1,\infty)
, and
\ell_{i}
is one of the following:

(a)

\ell_{1}(x)=(x-b)_{+}
with some
b\in\mathbb{R}
;

(b)

\ell_{2}(x)=(x-b)_{-}
with some
b\in\mathbb{R}
;

(c)

\ell_{3}(x)=\left(\left|x-b_{1}\right|-b_{2}\right)_{+}
with some
b_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
b_{2}\geqslant 0
;

(d)

\ell_{4}(x)=\left|x-b_{1}\right|+b_{2}
with some
b_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
b_{2}>0
.

The problem (
15
) can be solved by the convex program

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},v_{N+1}\geqslant 0,y\geqslant 0,z\geqslant 0,s}\left[z^{\top}b+C_{q}^{1}y+s\right]^{q}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~[A^{\top}z]_{i}\geqslant\frac{\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)}{y^{q-1}},~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~[A^{\top}z]_{N+1}\geqslant v_{N+1},

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~[A^{\top}z]_{N+2}\geqslant 0,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~s\geqslant C_{q}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{*}^{\frac{q}{q-1}},

where
C_{q}^{1}=q^{\frac{1}{1-q}}-q^{\frac{q}{1-q}}
and
C_{q}=C^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\left(q^{\frac{1}{1-q}}-q^{\frac{q}{1-q}}\right)
. And thus the problem (
15
) can be solved by the convex program

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},v_{N+1}\geqslant 0,y\geqslant 0,z\geqslant 0,s}z^{\top}b+C_{q}^{1}y+s

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~[A^{\top}z]_{i}\geqslant\frac{\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)}{y^{q-1}},~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~[A^{\top}z]_{N+1}\geqslant v_{N+1},

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~[A^{\top}z]_{N+2}\geqslant 0,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~s\geqslant C_{q}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{*}^{\frac{q}{q-1}}.

Note that the objective function of above reformulation is linear. Moreover, given
\alpha
, this formulation has
N+9
variables,
N+10
linear constraints and
N+1
convex constraint.

(iii)

Let
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}^{q}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
and
q\in(1,\infty)
. Assuming the loss function
\ell
is convex and satisfies
\ell(x)-\ell(x_{0})\leqslant L|x-x_{0}|^{q}+M,x\in\mathbb{R}
for some
L,M>0
, and some
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, the problem (
15
) can be solved by the convex program

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},\eta\geqslant 0,z\geqslant 0}~z^{\top}b

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~[A^{\top}z]_{i}\geqslant\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\},~i=1,...,N,

(44)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~[A^{\top}z]_{N+1}\geqslant||\alpha||_{*}^{q}/\eta^{q-1},

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~[A^{\top}z]_{N+2}\geqslant 0.

Note that the objective function of above reformulation is linear. Moreover, given
\alpha
, this formulation has
N+7
variables,
N+8
linear constraints and
N+1
convex constraint.

(B)

Note that partial optimal transport model (
5
) given by
Esteban-Perez and Morales 2022
is equivalent to problem (
15
) with set
\mathcal{V}
given in Theorem
6
. Applying the conjugate of the indicator function of
\mathcal{V}_{+}
in Example
3.3
to Propositions
10
and
11
, we have the following results.

(i)

Let
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
and
\ell:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a convex and Lipschitz continuous function.
The problem (
15
) can be solved by the convex program

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},z\geqslant 0}~z^{\top}b^{\prime}

\displaystyle~~~~~\text{s.t.}~\left[(A^{\prime})^{\top}z\right]_{i}\geqslant\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha),~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~\left[(A^{\prime})^{\top}z\right]_{N+1}\geqslant\operatorname{Lip}(\ell)||\alpha||_{*}.

(ii)

Let
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}^{q}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
and
q\in(1,\infty)
.

(1)

Assume the loss function
\ell
takes one of the following two forms, multiplied by
C>0
:

(a)

\ell_{1}(x)=x+b
or
\ell_{1}(x)=-x+b
with some
b\in\mathbb{R}
;

(b)

\ell_{2}(x)=\left|x-b_{1}\right|+b_{2}
with some
b_{1},b_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
.

The problem (
15
) can be solved by the convex program

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},v_{N+1}\geqslant 0,z\geqslant 0}~z^{\top}b^{\prime}+C_{q}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{*}^{\frac{q}{q-1}}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~[(A^{\prime})^{\top}z]_{i}\geqslant\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha),~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~[(A^{\prime})^{\top}z]_{N+1}\geqslant v_{N+1},

where
C_{q}=C^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\left(q^{\frac{1}{1-q}}-q^{\frac{q}{1-q}}\right)
.

(2)

Assume the loss function
\ell
is of the form
\ell(x)=\left(C\cdot\ell_{i}(x)\right)^{q}
, where
C>0
,
q\in(1,\infty)
, and
\ell_{i}
is one of the following:

(a)

\ell_{1}(x)=(x-b)_{+}
with some
b\in\mathbb{R}
;

(b)

\ell_{2}(x)=(x-b)_{-}
with some
b\in\mathbb{R}
;

(c)

\ell_{3}(x)=\left(\left|x-b_{1}\right|-b_{2}\right)_{+}
with some
b_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
b_{2}\geqslant 0
;

(d)

\ell_{4}(x)=\left|x-b_{1}\right|+b_{2}
with some
b_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
b_{2}>0
.

The problem (
15
) can be solved by the convex program

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},v_{N+1}\geqslant 0,y\geqslant 0,z\geqslant 0}\left[z^{\top}b^{\prime}+C_{q}^{1}y+C_{q}v_{N+1}\left\|\frac{\alpha}{v_{N+1}}\right\|_{*}^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\right]^{q}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~[(A^{\prime})^{\top}z]_{i}\geqslant\frac{\ell(\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha)}{y^{q-1}},~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~[(A^{\prime})^{\top}z]_{N+1}\geqslant v_{N+1},

where
C_{q}^{1}=q^{\frac{1}{1-q}}-q^{\frac{q}{1-q}}
and
C_{q}=C^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\left(q^{\frac{1}{1-q}}-q^{\frac{q}{1-q}}\right)
.

(iii)

Let
\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(y_{1},y_{2})=||y_{1}-y_{2}||_{\mathcal{Y}}^{q}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
and
q\in(1,\infty)
. Assuming the loss function
\ell
is convex and satisfies
\ell(x)-\ell(x_{0})\leqslant L|x-x_{0}|^{q}+M,x\in\mathbb{R}
for some
L,M>0
, and some
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}
, the problem (
15
) can be solved by the convex program

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A},\eta\geqslant 0,z\geqslant 0}~z^{\top}b^{\prime}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\text{s.t.}~[(A^{\prime})^{\top}z]_{i}\geqslant\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{z\cdot\widehat{y}_{i}^{\top}\alpha-\ell^{*}(z)+\eta\cdot\frac{|z|^{q^{*}}}{q^{*}q^{q^{*}-1}}\right\},~i=1,...,N,

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~[(A^{\prime})^{\top}z]_{N+1}\geqslant||\alpha||_{*}^{q}/\eta^{q-1}.

Remark 31
.

In what follows, we present a more detailed discussion of the full optimal transport model in comparison with the analysis of
Nguyen et al. 2024
. Specifically, for the case with expectation as the risk measure and
\mathcal{N}_{\omega}=[\varepsilon,1]
for some
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,
Nguyen et al. 2024
were the first to study this model and established the following equivalence:

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbb{Q}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta_{0}}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}})\\
\mathbb{Q}\left(X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}(x_{0})\right)\in[\varepsilon,1]\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\ell(Y,\alpha)\mid X\in\mathcal{N}_{\gamma}\left(x_{0}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\inf&\phi+(N\varepsilon)^{-1}\nu^{+}-N^{-1}\nu^{-}\\
\text{s.t. }&\alpha\in\mathcal{A},~\left(\lambda,s,\nu^{+},\nu^{-},\phi,\varphi,\psi\right)\in\mathcal{U}\\
&s_{i}\geqslant\sup_{{y}_{i}\in\mathcal{Y}}\left\{\ell\left({y}_{i},\alpha\right)-\lambda_{i}\mathbb{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}\left({y}_{i},\widehat{y}_{i}\right)\right\}\quad i=1,...,N,\end{cases}

where

\mathcal{U}\triangleq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(\lambda,s,\nu^{+},\nu^{-},\phi,\varphi,\psi\right)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{N}&\text{ such that: }\\
\phi+d_{i}\varphi+\psi_{i}-s_{i}\geqslant 0&i=1,...,m\\
\phi-d_{i}\varphi+\psi_{i}-s_{i}\geqslant 0&i=m+1,...,N\\
\nu^{+}-\nu^{-}+\left(\sum_{i=m+1}^{N}d_{i}-N\delta_{0}\right)\varphi-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\psi_{i}\geqslant 0&\\
\varphi-\lambda_{i}\geqslant 0&i=1,...,N\end{array}\right\}.

Note that the objective function of the above reformulation is linear. Moreover, given
\alpha
, this formulation has
3N+4
variables,
4N+4
linear constraints, and
N
convex constraints. A further comparison with
Nguyen et al. 2024
yields the following observations.

(i)

Although our formulations based on the projection property of
Wu et al. 2022
also involve the unsolved supremum problem in constraint (
44
), this problem is one-dimensional, whereas in
Nguyen et al. 2024
it is high-dimensional, resulting in substantially greater complexity.

(ii)

For specific loss functions—such as the mean–variance and mean–CVaR loss functions studied in
Nguyen et al. 2024
—the
N
supremum problems in the constraints (
44
) admit explicit forms, which reduce to SOC constraints. Compared with
Nguyen et al. 2024
, our reformulation is considerably simpler and more tractable, requiring simpler constraints (SOC constraints) while reformulation in
Nguyen et al. 2024
requires more complex constraint (SDP constraints). See Remark
16
for a detailed comparison under the mean–variance loss function with the squared
\ell_{2}
-norm, and Remark
23
for the corresponding comparison under the mean–CVaR loss function with the squared
\ell_{2}
-norm.

(iii)

Our reformulations are considerably simpler largely thanks to the union-ball characterization, which leverages the structural properties of standard Wasserstein DRO and requires invoking duality only once, introducing a single multiplier
\eta
. By contrast, the analysis in
Nguyen et al. 2024
, which overlooks this structural property and instead relies on a more cumbersome break-and-conquer approach, applies duality repeatedly—most clearly reflected in the
N
multipliers
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{N}
that grow with
N
.
